================================ Human Message =================================

泰坦尼克号的导演还导演过哪些电影？

================================ System Message ================================

你是一个具备工具调用能力的问答助手，可以检索知识库、搜索网页（若已启用）并回答用户问题。

检索策略：
- 优先用知识库检索（RAG_search_tool）获取证据。
- 若结果为空、与问题无关、明显过时，或问题很可能不在知识库内（如实时新闻、公开网页信息）：在网页搜索已启用时，应主动改用网页搜索（tavily_search），不要只做无效的内部重试。
- 可换 query 或调整检索参数重试；若多次仍不足，优先网页搜索而非强行作答。

每次回复的 **content**（面向用户的文本，不含 tool_calls 字段本身）必须严格使用下方模板，并用 ``` 代码块包裹：

```
Think:
- 根据当前对话与工具返回的信息，判断是否已经足够回答用户的问题。
- 若不足，说明缺什么、为什么还不够（例如：无命中、文段不相关、知识库可能不含该信息）。
- 概括接下来应该采取的策略（例如：换 query 重试检索、换检索参数、改用网页搜索、向用户澄清，或可以作答）。

Action:
- 若需要工具：明确写出即将调用的工具名称及目的（须与随后发起的 tool_calls 一致）。
- 若可以作答：明确写出「输出 final answer」，并在 Think 充分后在同一次或后续回复中给出最终答案。
- 若 retrieval gate 提示质量不足：不得输出 final answer；可改检索，或在网页搜索已启用时改用 tavily_search。
```

要求：
- 每一轮 content 都要包含完整的 Think 与 Action 两段（标题固定为英文 Think / Action），即使本轮只发起 tool_calls；除非当前模型无法同时输出 content 与 tool_calls，此时 content 可留空。
- 最终答案应基于已检索到的证据（知识库或网页）；证据不足时如实说明，不要编造。
- 作答尽量简洁、直接。

Click here for more information.\")\n\nFrom Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia\n\nCanadian filmmaker and explorer (born 1954)\n\nThis article is about the Canadian filmmaker. For other people, see [James Cameron (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron_(disambiguation) \"James Cameron (disambiguation)\").\n\n| James Cameron [CC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Canada \"Order of Canada\") |\n| --- |\n| [![Image 5](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/James_Cameron_at_53rd_Saturn_Awards_2026-01_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-James_Cameron_at_53rd_Saturn_Awards_2026-01_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:James_Cameron_at_53rd_Saturn_Awards_2026-01_(cropped).jpg) Cameron in 2026 |\n| Born | James Francis Cameron (1954-08-16) August 16, 1954 (age 71) [Kapuskasing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapuskasing \"Kapuskasing\"), Ontario, Canada |\n| Citizenship | * Canada ([by birth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jus_soli \"Jus soli\")) * New Zealand ([acquired](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_nationality_law#Voluntary_acquisition \"New Zealand nationality law\"), since 2025) |\n| Occupations | * Film director * film producer * screenwriter * editor * artist * philanthropist * [deep-sea explorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep-sea_exploration \"Deep-sea exploration\") |\n| Years active | 1978–present |\n| Works | * [Filmography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron_filmography \"James Cameron filmography\") * [unrealized projects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron%27s_unrealized_projects \"James Cameron's unrealized projects\") |\n| Spouses | * Sharon Williams ​ ​ (m.1978; div.1984)​ [Gale Anne Hurd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gale_Anne_Hurd \"Gale Anne Hurd\") ​ ​ (m.1985; div.1989)​ [Kathryn Bigelow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathryn_Bigelow \"Kathryn Bigelow\") ​ ​ (m.1989; div.1991)​ [Linda Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linda_Hamilton \"Linda Hamilton\") ​ ​ (m.1997; div.1999)​ [Suzy Amis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzy_Amis_Cameron \"Suzy Amis Cameron\") ​ (m.2000)​ |\n| Children | 5[[1]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-1) |\n| Awards | [Full list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_James_Cameron \"List of awards and nominations received by James Cameron\") |\n| Signature |\n| [![Image 6](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/James_Cameron_Signature.svg/250px-James_Cameron_Signature.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:James_Cameron_Signature.svg) |\n\n**James Francis Cameron** (born August 16, 1954) is a Canadian filmmaker and [deep-sea explorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep-sea_exploration \"Deep-sea exploration\"). [His films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron_filmography \"James Cameron filmography\") combine cutting-edge [film technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_film_technology \"History of film technology\") with [classical filmmaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Hollywood_cinema \"Classical Hollywood cinema\") techniques and have grossed over $10 billion worldwide, making him the [second highest-grossing film director](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_film_directors \"List of highest-grossing film directors\") of all time. A major figure in the post-[New Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Hollywood \"New Hollywood\") era, Cameron has received [numerous accolades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_nominations_received_by_James_Cameron \"List of awards and nominations received by James Cameron\") including three [Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Awards \"Academy Awards\"), two [Primetime Emmy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primetime_Emmy_Awards \"Primetime Emmy Awards\"), and four [Golden Globe Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Awards \"Golden Globe Awards\"), as well as nominations for six [British Academy Film Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Academy_Film_Awards \"British Academy Film Awards\").\n\nBorn and raised in [Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ontario \"Ontario\"), Cameron moved to California aged 17 and enrolled at [Fullerton Community College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fullerton_Community_College \"Fullerton Community College\"). Beginning his career with the short film _[Xenogenesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xenogenesis\\_(film) \"Xenogenesis (film)\")_ (1978), he first gained recognition for writing and directing the science fiction action film _[The Terminator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Terminator \"The Terminator\")_ (1984). He had further success with _[Aliens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliens\\_(film) \"Aliens (film)\")_ (1986), _[The Abyss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Abyss \"The Abyss\")_ (1989), _[Terminator 2: Judgment Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminator\\_2:\\_Judgment\\_Day \"Terminator 2: Judgment Day\")_ (1991), and _[True Lies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True\\_Lies \"True Lies\")_ (1994), as well as the [_Avatar_ franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar_(franchise) \"Avatar (franchise)\") (2009, 2022–present). He directed, wrote, co-produced, and co-edited the historical romance epic _[Titanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanic\\_(1997\\_film) \"Titanic (1997 film)\")_ (1997), winning [Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Awards \"Academy Awards\") for [Best Picture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Picture \"Academy Award for Best Picture\"), [Best Director](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Director \"Academy Award for Best Director\"), and [Best Film Editing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Film_Editing \"Academy Award for Best Film Editing\").\n\nThree of Cameron's films—_[Avatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar\\_(2009\\_film) \"Avatar (2009 film)\")_ (2009), _[Avatar: The Way of Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar:\\_The\\_Way\\_of\\_Water \"Avatar: The Way of Water\")_ (2022) and _Titanic_—are amongst the top four [highest-grossing films of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_films \"List of highest-grossing films\").[[2]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-2) He directed the first film to gross over $1 billion, the first two films to gross over $2 billion each, is the only director to have had three films gross over $2 billion each, and is the first director to have four consecutive feature films gross over $1 billion each.[[3]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-3)[[4]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-4)[[5]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-5)[[6]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-6)_The Terminator_, _Terminator 2: Judgment Day_, and _Titanic_ have been selected for preservation in the [National Film Registry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Film_Registry \"National Film Registry\") by the [Library of Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Library_of_Congress \"Library of Congress\"). Cameron also co-founded the production companies [Lightstorm Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lightstorm_Entertainment \"Lightstorm Entertainment\"), [Digital Domain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_Domain \"Digital Domain\"), and Earthship Productions. In 2010, _[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time\\_(magazine) \"Time (magazine)\")_ named Cameron one of the [100 most influential people in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100#2010s \"Time 100\").\n\nIn addition to filmmaking, he is a [National Geographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic \"National Geographic\") explorer-in-residence and has produced many documentaries on deep-ocean exploration, including _[Ghosts of the Abyss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghosts\\_of\\_the\\_Abyss \"Ghosts of the Abyss\")_ (2003) and _[Aliens of the Deep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliens\\_of\\_the\\_Deep \"Aliens of the Deep\")_ (2005). Cameron has also contributed to [underwater filming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underwater_videography \"Underwater videography\") and [remote vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remotely_operated_underwater_vehicle \"Remotely operated underwater vehicle\") technologies, and helped create the new [digital 3D](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_3D \"Digital 3D\")[Fusion Camera System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fusion_Camera_System \"Fusion Camera System\"). In 2012, he became the first person to complete a solo descent to the bottom of the [Mariana Trench](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariana_Trench \"Mariana Trench\"), the deepest part of Earth's ocean, in the _[Deepsea Challenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deepsea\\_Challenger \"Deepsea Challenger\")_ submersible. He is also an environmentalist and runs several sustainability businesses.\n\n## Early life and education\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=1 \"Edit section: Early life and education\")]\n\nJames Francis Cameron[[7]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-7) was born on August 16, 1954, in [Kapuskasing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapuskasing \"Kapuskasing\"), Ontario, to Philip Cameron, an [electrical engineer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrical_engineer \"Electrical engineer\"), and Shirley ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_name#Maiden_and_married_names \"Birth name\")Lowe), an artist and nurse.[[8]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-nytimesref-8) He is the first of five children, with two brothers and two sisters.[[8]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-nytimesref-8) His paternal great-great-great-grandfather emigrated from [Balquhidder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balquhidder \"Balquhidder\"), Scotland, in 1825.[[8]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-nytimesref-8) Cameron spent summers on his grandfather's farm in [Southern Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Ontario \"Southern Ontario\").[[9]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-9) He attended Stamford Collegiate in [Niagara Falls, Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niagara_Falls,_Ontario \"Niagara Falls, Ontario\"). At age 17, Cameron and his family moved from [Chippawa, Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chippawa,_Ontario \"Chippawa, Ontario\") to [Brea, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brea,_California \"Brea, California\").[[10]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-10) He attended [Sonora High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonora_High_School_(La_Habra,_California) \"Sonora High School (La Habra, California)\") and then moved to [Brea Olinda High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brea_Olinda_High_School \"Brea Olinda High School\"). Classmates recalled that he was not a sportsman but instead enjoyed building things that \"either went up into the air or into the deep\".[[11]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-11)\n\nAfter high school, Cameron enrolled at [Fullerton College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fullerton_College \"Fullerton College\"), a community college, in 1973 to study physics. He switched subjects to English, but left the college at the end of 1974.[[12]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-12) Cameron worked odd jobs, including as a truck driver and a high school janitor. He drank beer, frequently consumed [cannabis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannabis_(drug) \"Cannabis (drug)\") and [LSD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LSD \"LSD\"), and wrote in his free time.[[13]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-13)[[14]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-filmmakers.com-14) During this period, he learned about special effects by reading other students' work on \"[optical printing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optical_printer \"Optical printer\"), or [front screen projection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_projection_effect \"Front projection effect\"), or [dye transfers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dye-transfer_process \"Dye-transfer process\"), anything that related to [film technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_film_technology \"History of film technology\")\" at the [University of Southern California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Southern_California \"University of Southern California\") library.[[15]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Syd_Field-15) After the excitement of seeing _[Star Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star\\_Wars\\_(film) \"Star Wars (film)\")_ in 1977, Cameron quit his job as a truck driver to enter the [film industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_industry \"Film industry\").[[16]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-16)\n\n## Career\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=2 \"Edit section: Career\")]\n\n### 1978–1989: Early career and breakthrough\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=3 \"Edit section: 1978–1989: Early career and breakthrough\")]\n\n[![Image 7: Cameron in September 1986](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/James_Cameron.jpg/250px-James_Cameron.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:James_Cameron.jpg)\n\nCameron, September 1986\n\nCameron's directing career began in 1978.[[17]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-17) After borrowing money from a consortium of dentists, he learned to direct, write and produce his first short film, _[Xenogenesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xenogenesis\\_(film) \"Xenogenesis (film)\")_ (1978), with a friend.[[18]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-18) Learning as he went, Cameron said he felt like a doctor doing his first surgical procedure.[[15]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Syd_Field-15) He then served as a production assistant for _[Rock 'n' Roll High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock\\_%27n%27\\_Roll\\_High\\_School \"Rock 'n' Roll High School\")_ (1979). While educating himself about filmmaking techniques, Cameron started a job as a miniature [model maker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Model_maker \"Model maker\") at [Roger Corman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Corman \"Roger Corman\") Studios.[[14]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-filmmakers.com-14)[[19]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-19) He was soon employed as an art director for the science-fiction film _[Battle Beyond the Stars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle\\_Beyond\\_the\\_Stars \"Battle Beyond the Stars\")_ (1980). He carried out the special effects for [John Carpenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Carpenter \"John Carpenter\")'s _[Escape from New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escape\\_from\\_New\\_York \"Escape from New York\")_ (1981), served as production designer for _[Galaxy of Terror](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galaxy\\_of\\_Terror \"Galaxy of Terror\")_ (1981), and consulted on the design for _[Android](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Android\\_(film) \"Android (film)\")_ (1982).\n\nCameron was hired as the visual effects director for the sequel to _[Piranha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piranha\\_(1978\\_film) \"Piranha (1978 film)\")_ (1978), titled _[Piranha II: The Spawning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piranha\\_II:\\_The\\_Spawning \"Piranha II: The Spawning\")_ in 1982. The original director, Miller Drake, left the project due to creative differences with producer [Ovidio Assonitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ovidio_Assonitis \"Ovidio Assonitis\"). Shot in Rome, Italy, and on [Grand Cayman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Cayman \"Grand Cayman\") Island, the film gave Cameron the opportunity to become director for a major film for the first time. Cameron later said that it did not feel like his first film due to power-struggles with Assonitis.[[20]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-20) Upon release of _Piranha II: The Spawning_, critics were not impressed; author Tim Healey called it \"a marvellously bad movie which splices clichés from every conceivable source\".[[21]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-21)\n\nIn 1982, inspired by [John Carpenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Carpenter \"John Carpenter\")'s horror film _[Halloween](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halloween\\_(1978\\_film) \"Halloween (1978 film)\")_ (1978),[[22]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-22) as well as a nightmare about an invincible robot hit-man sent from the future to assassinate him,[[23]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-23) Cameron wrote the script for _[The Terminator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Terminator \"The Terminator\")_ (1984), a sci-fi action film about a [cyborg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyborg \"Cyborg\") sent from the future to carry out a lethal mission. Cameron wanted to sell the script so that he could direct the film. While some film studios expressed interest in the project, many executives were unwilling to let a new and unfamiliar director make the film. [Gale Anne Hurd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gale_Anne_Hurd \"Gale Anne Hurd\"), a colleague and founder of [Pacific Western Productions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valhalla_Entertainment \"Valhalla Entertainment\"), agreed to buy Cameron's script for one dollar, on the condition that Cameron direct the film. He convinced the president of [Hemdale Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hemdale_Film_Corporation \"Hemdale Film Corporation\") to make the film, with Cameron as director and Hurd as a producer. [Lance Henriksen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lance_Henriksen \"Lance Henriksen\"), who starred in _Piranha II: The Spawning_, was considered for the lead role, but Cameron decided that [Arnold Schwarzenegger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnold_Schwarzenegger \"Arnold Schwarzenegger\") was more suitable as the cyborg villain due to his [bodybuilder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bodybuilder \"Bodybuilder\") appearance.[[24]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Futurist-24) Henriksen was given a smaller role instead. [Michael Biehn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Biehn \"Michael Biehn\") and [Linda Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linda_Hamilton \"Linda Hamilton\") also joined the cast. _The Terminator_ was a box office success, exceeding expectations set by [Orion Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orion_Pictures \"Orion Pictures\"),[[24]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Futurist-24) and earning over $78 million worldwide.[[25]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-25) George Perry of the [BBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC \"BBC\") praised Cameron's direction, writing: \"Cameron laces the action with ironic jokes, but never lets up on hinting that the terror may strike at any moment\".[[26]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-26) In 2008, the film was selected for preservation in the United States [National Film Registry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Film_Registry \"National Film Registry\"), being deemed \"culturally, historically, or aesthetically significant\".[[27]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-27)\n\nIn 1984, Cameron was hired to write a sequel to _[First Blood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First\\_Blood \"First Blood\")_; it was rewritten by [Sylvester Stallone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylvester_Stallone \"Sylvester Stallone\") and released as _[Rambo: First Blood Part II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rambo:\\_First\\_Blood\\_Part\\_II \"Rambo: First Blood Part II\")_.[[28]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-28)[[29]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-29) Cameron was then hired to write and direct a sequel to _[Alien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alien\\_(film) \"Alien (film)\")_ (1979), a science fiction horror film directed by [Ridley Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ridley_Scott \"Ridley Scott\"). Like the original, the sequel _[Aliens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliens\\_(film) \"Aliens (film)\")_ (1986) featured [Sigourney Weaver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigourney_Weaver \"Sigourney Weaver\") as [Ellen Ripley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellen_Ripley \"Ellen Ripley\"). _Aliens_ follows Ripley as she helps a group of marines fight off [extraterrestrials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extraterrestrial_life \"Extraterrestrial life\"). Despite conflicts with cast and crew during production, and having to replace one of the lead actors—[James Remar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Remar \"James Remar\") with [Michael Biehn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Biehn \"Michael Biehn\")—_Aliens_ was a box office success, generating over $130 million worldwide.[[30]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-30) The film was nominated for seven [Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Awards \"Academy Awards\") in 1987; [Best Actress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Actress \"Academy Award for Best Actress\"), [Best Art Direction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Production_Design \"Academy Award for Best Production Design\"), [Best Film Editing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Film_Editing \"Academy Award for Best Film Editing\"), [Best Original Score](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Original_Score \"Academy Award for Best Original Score\") and [Best Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Sound_Mixing \"Academy Award for Best Sound Mixing\"). It won awards for [Best Sound Editing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Sound_Editing \"Academy Award for Best Sound Editing\") and [Best Visual Effects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Visual_Effects \"Academy Award for Best Visual Effects\").[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-31) In addition, Weaver and the film made the cover of _[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time\\_(magazine) \"Time (magazine)\")_ in July 1986.[[32]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-32)\n\n[![Image 8: Cameron with Gale Anne Hurd, 1986](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Gale_Ann_Hurd_and_James_Cameron.jpg/250px-Gale_Ann_Hurd_and_James_Cameron.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gale_Ann_Hurd_and_James_Cameron.jpg)\n\nCameron with Gale Anne Hurd, 1986\n\nAfter _Aliens_, Cameron and Gale Anne Hurd decided to make _[The Abyss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Abyss \"The Abyss\")_, a story about oil-rig workers who discover strange intelligent life in the ocean. Based on an idea which Cameron had conceived of during high school, the film was initially budgeted at $41 million, although it ran considerably over this amount. It starred [Ed Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Harris \"Ed Harris\"), [Mary Elizabeth Mastrantonio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Elizabeth_Mastrantonio \"Mary Elizabeth Mastrantonio\") and [Michael Biehn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Biehn \"Michael Biehn\"). The production process began in the Cayman Islands and in [South Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Carolina \"South Carolina\"), in two huge water tanks \"reclaimed from\" an unfinished [nuclear power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power \"Nuclear power\") plant.[[33]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-33) The cast and crew recall Cameron's dictatorial behavior, and the filming of water scenes which were mentally and physically exhausting.[[34]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Harmetz-1989-34) Upon the film's release, _The Abyss_ was praised for its special effects, and earned $90 million at the worldwide box office.[[35]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-35)_The Abyss_ received four Academy Award nominations, and won Best Visual Effects.[[36]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-36)\n\n### 1990–1999: Stardom and acclaim\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=4 \"Edit section: 1990–1999: Stardom and acclaim\")]\n\nIn 1990, Cameron co-founded the firm [Lightstorm Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lightstorm_Entertainment \"Lightstorm Entertainment\") with collaborator [Lawrence Kasanoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence_Kasanoff \"Lawrence Kasanoff\"). In 1991, Cameron served as executive producer for _[Point Break](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point\\_Break \"Point Break\")_ (1991), directed by [Kathryn Bigelow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathryn_Bigelow \"Kathryn Bigelow\"). After the success of _The Terminator_, there were discussions for a sequel, and by the late 1980s, [Mario Kassar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Kassar \"Mario Kassar\") of [Carolco Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolco_Pictures \"Carolco Pictures\") secured the rights to the sequel, allowing Cameron to begin production of the film, _[Terminator 2: Judgment Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminator\\_2:\\_Judgment\\_Day \"Terminator 2: Judgment Day\")_ (1991). Written by Cameron and [William Wisher Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Wisher_Jr. \"William Wisher Jr.\"), Schwarzenegger and Linda Hamilton reprise their roles. The story follows on from _Terminator_, depicting a new villain ([T-1000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-1000 \"T-1000\")), with [shape-shifting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shapeshifting \"Shapeshifting\") abilities who hunts for [Sarah Connor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Connor_(Terminator) \"Sarah Connor (Terminator)\")'s son, John ([Edward Furlong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Furlong \"Edward Furlong\")). Cameron cast [Robert Patrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Patrick \"Robert Patrick\") as T-1000 because of his lean and thin appearance—a sharp contrast to Schwarzenegger. Cameron explained: \"I wanted someone who was extremely fast and agile. If the [T-800](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminator_(character) \"Terminator (character)\") is a human [Panzer tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanks_in_the_German_Army \"Tanks in the German Army\"), then the T-1000 is a [Porsche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porsche \"Porsche\")\".[[37]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-37)_Terminator 2_ was one of the most expensive films to be produced, costing at least $94 million[[38]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-38) ($222 million in 2025[[39]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-inflation-US-39)). Despite the challenging use of [computer-generated imagery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer-generated_imagery \"Computer-generated imagery\") (CGI), the film was completed on time and released on July 3, 1991. _Terminator 2_ broke box office records (including the opening weekend record for an [R-rated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_Picture_Association_film_rating_system \"Motion Picture Association film rating system\") film), earning over $200 million in North America and being the first to earn over $300 million worldwide[[40]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-40) (respectively over $473 million and $709 million in 2025[[39]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-inflation-US-39)). It won four Academy Awards: [Best Makeup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Makeup_and_Hairstyling \"Academy Award for Best Makeup and Hairstyling\"), [Best Sound Mixing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Sound \"Academy Award for Best Sound\"), Best Sound Editing and Best Visual Effects. It also received nominations for [Best Cinematography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Cinematography \"Academy Award for Best Cinematography\") and [Best Film Editing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Film_Editing \"Academy Award for Best Film Editing\").[[41]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-41)\n\nIn subsequent years, Cameron planned to do a third _Terminator_ film, but plans never materialized. The rights to the [_Terminator_ franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminator_(franchise) \"Terminator (franchise)\") were eventually purchased by Kassar from a bankruptcy sale of Carolco's assets.[[42]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-42) Cameron moved on to other projects and, in 1993, co-founded [Digital Domain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_Domain \"Digital Domain\"), a visual effects production company. In 1994, Cameron and Schwarzenegger reunited for their third collaboration, _[True Lies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True\\_Lies \"True Lies\"),_ a remake of the 1991 French comedy _[La Totale!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La\\_Totale! \"La Totale!\")_. The story depicts an American secret agent who leads a double life as a married man, whose wife believes he is a computer salesman. The film co-stars [Jamie Lee Curtis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamie_Lee_Curtis \"Jamie Lee Curtis\"), [Eliza Dushku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eliza_Dushku \"Eliza Dushku\") and [Tom Arnold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Arnold_(actor) \"Tom Arnold (actor)\"). Cameron's Lightstorm Entertainment signed a deal with [20th Century Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th_Century_Fox \"20th Century Fox\") for the production of _True Lies_. Budgeted at a minimum of $100 million, the film earned $146 million in the United States and Canada.[[43]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-43)[[44]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-44) The film was nominated for an Academy Award for Best Visual Effects and Curtis won a [Golden Globe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Awards \"Golden Globe Awards\") Award for [Best Actress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe_Award_for_Best_Actress_%E2%80%93_Motion_Picture_Comedy_or_Musical \"Golden Globe Award for Best Actress – Motion Picture Comedy or Musical\").[[45]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-45)[[46]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-46) It was during the production of _True Lies_ that he would first meet [Jon Landau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Landau_(film_producer) \"Jon Landau (film producer)\"), who at the time oversaw the film's production for Fox.[[47]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-cameronspeaks-47) In July 2024, Cameron stated that he \"lured\" Landau away from Fox to Lightstorm.[[47]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-cameronspeaks-47)\n\nIn 1995, Cameron co-produced [_Strange Days_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strange_Days_(film) \"Strange Days (film)\"), a science fiction thriller. Directed by Kathryn Bigelow and co-written by [Jay Cocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jay_Cocks \"Jay Cocks\"), _Strange Days_ was critically and financially unsuccessful.[[48]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-48) In 1996, Cameron reunited with the cast of _Terminator 2_ to film _[T2 3-D: Battle Across Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T2\\_3-D:\\_Battle\\_Across\\_Time \"T2 3-D: Battle Across Time\")_, an attraction at [Universal Studios Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Studios_Florida \"Universal Studios Florida\"), and in other parks around the world.[[49]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-49)\n\nHis next major project was [_Titanic_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanic_(1997_film) \"Titanic (1997 film)\") (1997), an [epic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_film \"Epic film\") about the [RMS _Titanic_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanic \"Titanic\"), which [sank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinking_of_the_Titanic \"Sinking of the Titanic\") in 1912 after hitting an iceberg. With a production budget of $200 million, at the time it was the [most expensive film ever made](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_films \"List of most expensive films\"). Starting in 1995, Cameron took several dives to the bottom of the [Atlantic Ocean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean \"Atlantic Ocean\") to capture footage of the [wreck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wreck_of_the_Titanic \"Wreck of the Titanic\"), which would later be used in the film.[[50]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-50) A replica of the ship was built in [Rosarito Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosarito_Beach \"Rosarito Beach\") and [principal photography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principal_photography \"Principal photography\") began in September 1996. _Titanic_ made headlines before its release, for being over-budget and exceeding its schedule.[[51]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-51)[[52]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-52) In a radical departure from Cameron's previous work, his completed screenplay depicts two [star-crossed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star-crossed \"Star-crossed\") lovers, portrayed by [Leonardo DiCaprio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_DiCaprio \"Leonardo DiCaprio\") and [Kate Winslet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Winslet \"Kate Winslet\"), from different social classes who fall in love amid the backdrop of the ship's tragedy. The supporting cast includes [Billy Zane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billy_Zane \"Billy Zane\"), [Kathy Bates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathy_Bates \"Kathy Bates\"), [Frances Fisher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frances_Fisher \"Frances Fisher\"), [Gloria Stuart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloria_Stuart \"Gloria Stuart\"), [Bernard Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Hill \"Bernard Hill\"), [Jonathan Hyde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Hyde \"Jonathan Hyde\"), [Victor Garber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Garber \"Victor Garber\"), [Danny Nucci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Nucci \"Danny Nucci\"), [David Warner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Warner_(actor) \"David Warner (actor)\") and [Bill Paxton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Paxton \"Bill Paxton\"). The film was also Cameron's first large-scale production with Landau as a co-producer.[[47]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-cameronspeaks-47)\n\nAfter months of delay, _Titanic_ premiered on December 19, 1997. The film received strong critical acclaim and became the [highest-grossing film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_films \"List of highest-grossing films\") of all time, holding this position for twelve years, until Cameron's _[Avatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar\\_(2009\\_film) \"Avatar (2009 film)\")_ beat the record in 2010.[[53]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-53)[[54]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-54)[[55]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-55) The costumes and sets were praised, and _[The Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Washington\\_Post \"The Washington Post\")_ considered the CGI graphics to be spectacular.[[56]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Keller-2014-56)[[57]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-57)_Titanic_ received a record-tying fourteen nominations (tied with _[All About Eve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All\\_About\\_Eve \"All About Eve\")_ in 1950) at the [1998 Academy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/70th_Academy_Awards \"70th Academy Awards\"). It won eleven of the awards, tying the record for most wins with 1959's _[Ben-Hur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben-Hur\\_(1959\\_film) \"Ben-Hur (1959 film)\")_ (now also tied with 2003's _[The Lord of the Rings: The Return of the King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Lord\\_of\\_the\\_Rings:\\_The\\_Return\\_of\\_the\\_King \"The Lord of the Rings: The Return of the King\")_) including: [Best Picture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Picture \"Academy Award for Best Picture\"), [Best Director](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Director \"Academy Award for Best Director\"), Best Art Direction, Best Cinematography, Best Visual Effects, Best Film Editing, [Best Costume Design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Costume_Design \"Academy Award for Best Costume Design\"), Best Sound Mixing, Best Sound Editing, Best Original Score and [Best Original Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Original_Song \"Academy Award for Best Original Song\").[[58]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-58) Upon receiving Best Picture, Cameron and producer [Jon Landau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Landau_(film_producer) \"Jon Landau (film producer)\") asked for a moment of silence to remember the 1,500 people who died when the ship sank.[[59]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-59) Film critic [Roger Ebert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Ebert \"Roger Ebert\") praised Cameron's storytelling, writing: \"It is flawlessly crafted, intelligently constructed, strongly acted, and spellbinding\".[[60]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-60) Authors Kevin Sandler and [Gaylyn Studlar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaylyn_Studlar \"Gaylyn Studlar\") wrote in 1999 that the romance, historical nostalgia and [James Horner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Horner \"James Horner\")'s music contributed to the film's cultural phenomenon.[[61]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-61) In 2017, on its 20th anniversary, _Titanic_ became Cameron's second film to be selected for preservation in the United States National Film Registry.[[62]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-62)\n\nAfter the huge success of _Titanic_, Cameron kept a low profile. In 1998, he and his brother, John, formed Earthship Productions, to [stream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streaming_media \"Streaming media\") documentaries about the [deep sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_sea \"Deep sea\"), one of Cameron's interests.[[63]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Seering-63)[[64]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-64) Again during 1998, Cameron considered doing a large-scale technological/religious film by an unknown writer, but after three tries was forced to personally pass on the project \"due to his secular nature.\"[[65]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-65) Cameron had also planned to make a film about [Spider-Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spider-Man \"Spider-Man\"), a project developed by [Menahem Golan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menahem_Golan \"Menahem Golan\") of [Cannon Films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannon_Films \"Cannon Films\"). Columbia hired [David Koepp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Koepp \"David Koepp\") to adapt Cameron's ideas into a screenplay, but due to various disagreements, Cameron abandoned the project.[[66]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-66) In 2002, _[Spider-Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spider-Man\\_(2002\\_film) \"Spider-Man (2002 film)\")_ was released, with the screenplay credited solely to Koepp.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-67)\n\n### 2000–2009: Established career\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=5 \"Edit section: 2000–2009: Established career\")]\n\nIn 2000, Cameron made his debut in television and, with [Charles H. Eglee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_H._Eglee \"Charles H. Eglee\"), co-created _[Dark Angel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dark\\_Angel\\_(2000\\_TV\\_series) \"Dark Angel (2000 TV series)\")_, a television series influenced by [cyberpunk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyberpunk \"Cyberpunk\"), [biopunk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biopunk \"Biopunk\"), contemporary superheroes and [third-wave feminism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third-wave_feminism \"Third-wave feminism\"). _Dark Angel_ starred [Jessica Alba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jessica_Alba \"Jessica Alba\") as [Max Guevara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Guevara \"Max Guevara\"), a [genetically enhanced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetic_engineering \"Genetic engineering\") super-soldier created by a secretive organization. While the first season was moderately successful, the second season did less well, which led to its cancellation.[[68]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-68)\n\nIn 2002, Cameron served as producer on the 2002 film _[Solaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solaris\\_(2002\\_film) \"Solaris (2002 film)\")_, a science fiction drama directed by [Steven Soderbergh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Soderbergh \"Steven Soderbergh\"). The film gained mixed reviews and failed at the box office.[[69]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-69)[[70]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-70) Keen to make documentaries, Cameron directed _[Expedition: Bismarck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expedition:\\_Bismarck \"Expedition: Bismarck\")_, about the German Battleship _[Bismarck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German\\_battleship\\_Bismarck \"German battleship Bismarck\")_. In 2003, he directed _Ghosts of the Abyss_, a documentary about RMS _Titanic_ which was released by [Walt Disney Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney_Pictures \"Walt Disney Pictures\") and [Walden Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walden_Media \"Walden Media\"), and designed for [3D theaters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3D_film \"3D film\"). Cameron told _[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Guardian \"The Guardian\")_ his intention for filming everything in 3D.[[71]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-71) In 2005, Cameron co-directed _[Aliens of the Deep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliens\\_of\\_the\\_Deep \"Aliens of the Deep\")_, a documentary about the various forms of life in the ocean. He also starred in _Titanic Adventure_ with [Tony Robinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Robinson \"Tony Robinson\"), another documentary about the _Titanic_ shipwreck. In 2006, Cameron co-created and narrated _[The Exodus Decoded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Exodus\\_Decoded \"The Exodus Decoded\")_, a documentary exploring the [Biblical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible \"Bible\") account of [the Exodus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Exodus \"The Exodus\"). In 2007, Cameron and fellow director [Simcha Jacobovici](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simcha_Jacobovici \"Simcha Jacobovici\"), produced _[The Lost Tomb of Jesus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Lost\\_Tomb\\_of\\_Jesus \"The Lost Tomb of Jesus\")_. It was broadcast on [Discovery Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discovery_Channel \"Discovery Channel\") on March 4, 2007; the documentary was controversial for arguing that the [Talpiot Tomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talpiot_Tomb \"Talpiot Tomb\") was the burial place of Jesus of Nazareth.[[72]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-72)[[73]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-73)\n\nBy the mid-2000s, Cameron returned to directing and producing his first mainstream film since _Titanic_. Cameron had displayed interest in making _[Avatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar\\_(2009\\_film) \"Avatar (2009 film)\")_ (2009) and _[Alita: Battle Angel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alita:\\_Battle\\_Angel \"Alita: Battle Angel\")_ (2019) as early as June 2005, with both films to be shot using 3D technology.[[74]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-74) He wanted to make _Alita: Battle Angel_ first, followed by _Avatar_, but switched the order in February 2006. Although Cameron had written an 80-page [treatment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scriptment \"Scriptment\") for _Avatar_ in 1995, Cameron stated that he wanted the necessary technology to improve before starting production.[[75]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-75)[[76]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-76)_Avatar_, with the story line set in the mid-22nd century, had an estimated budget in excess of $300 million. The cast includes [Sam Worthington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Worthington \"Sam Worthington\"), [Zoe Saldaña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoe_Salda%C3%B1a \"Zoe Saldaña\"), [Stephen Lang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Lang \"Stephen Lang\"), [Michelle Rodriguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelle_Rodriguez \"Michelle Rodriguez\") and [Sigourney Weaver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigourney_Weaver \"Sigourney Weaver\"). It was composed with a mix of live-action footage and computer-generated animation, using an advanced version of the [motion capture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_capture \"Motion capture\") technique, previously used by director [Robert Zemeckis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Zemeckis \"Robert Zemeckis\") in _[The Polar Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Polar\\_Express\\_(film) \"The Polar Express (film)\")_.[[77]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-77) Cameron intended _Avatar_ to be 3D-only but decided to adapt it for conventional viewing as well.[[78]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-78)\n\n[![Image 9: Cameron promoting Avatar during the 2009 San Diego Comic-Con](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/JamesCameronCCJuly09.jpg/250px-JamesCameronCCJuly09.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:JamesCameronCCJuly09.jpg)\n\nCameron promoting _Avatar_ at [San Diego Comic-Con](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_Comic-Con \"San Diego Comic-Con\"), 2009\n\nIntended for release in May 2009, _Avatar_ premiered on December 18, 2009. This delay allowed more time for [post-production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-production \"Post-production\") and the opportunity for theaters to install 3D projectors.[[79]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-79)_Avatar_ broke several box office records during its initial theatrical run. It grossed $749.7 million in the United States and Canada and more than $2.74 billion worldwide, becoming the highest-grossing film of all time in the [United States and Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_films_in_Canada_and_the_United_States \"List of highest-grossing films in Canada and the United States\"), surpassing _Titanic_.[[80]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-80) It was the first film to earn more than $2 billion worldwide. _Avatar_ was nominated for nine Academy Awards, including Best Picture and Best Director, and won three: Best Art Direction, Best Cinematography and Best Visual Effects.[[81]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-81) In July 2010, an extended theatrical re-release generated an additional $33.2 million worldwide (equivalent to $46,480,000 in 2024) at the box office. In his mixed review, Sukhdev Sandhu of [_The Telegraph_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Telegraph \"The Daily Telegraph\") complimented the 3D, but opined that Cameron \"should have been more brutal in his editing\".[[82]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-82) That year, _[Vanity Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanity\\_Fair\\_(magazine) \"Vanity Fair (magazine)\")_ reported that Cameron's earnings were US$257 million, making him the highest earner in Hollywood.[[83]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-83) As of 2022, _Avatar_ and _Titanic_ hold the achievement for being the first two of the six films in history to gross over $2 billion worldwide.[[84]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-84) As with _Titanic_, Landau would greatly assist Cameron as the co-producer of the _Avatar_ films as well.[[47]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-cameronspeaks-47)\n\n### 2010–present: Further work and prospective projects\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=6 \"Edit section: 2010–present: Further work and prospective projects\")]\n\nIn June 2010, Cameron met with officials of the [Environmental Protection Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Environmental_Protection_Agency \"United States Environmental Protection Agency\") to discuss possible solutions to the [Deepwater Horizon oil spill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deepwater_Horizon_oil_spill \"Deepwater Horizon oil spill\"). It was reported that he offered his assistance to help stop the oil well from leaking.[[85]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-guardian2010-85)[[86]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-86) He is a member of the [NASA Advisory Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASA_Advisory_Council \"NASA Advisory Council\") and he worked with the space agency to build cameras for the [Curiosity rover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curiosity_(rover) \"Curiosity (rover)\") sent for [Mars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mars \"Mars\").[[87]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-87) NASA launched the rover without Cameron's technology due to a lack of time during testing.[[88]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-88) He has expressed interest in a project about Mars, stating: \"I've been very interested in the Humans to Mars movement ... and I've done a tremendous amount of personal research for a novel, a miniseries, and a 3D film.\"[[89]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-89) Cameron is a member of the [Mars Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mars_Society \"Mars Society\"), a non-profit organization lobbying for the [colonization of Mars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonization_of_Mars \"Colonization of Mars\").[[90]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-90)[[91]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-91) Cameron endorsed Democratic candidate [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton \"Hillary Clinton\") for the [2016 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election \"2016 United States presidential election\").[[92]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-92)\n\nIn 2011, Cameron served as an executive producer for _[Sanctum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctum\\_(film) \"Sanctum (film)\")_, a disaster-survival film about a [cave diving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cave_diving \"Cave diving\") expedition which turns deadly. Although receiving mixed reviews, the film earned a fair $108 million at the worldwide box office.[[93]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-93) Cameron re-investigated the sinking of RMS _Titanic_ with eight experts in a 2012 TV documentary special, _Titanic: The Final Word with James Cameron_, which premiered on April 8 on the [National Geographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic_(American_TV_channel) \"National Geographic (American TV channel)\") channel.[[94]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-94) In the feature, the experts revised the CGI animation of the sinking conceived in 1995.[[95]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-95)[[96]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-96) In March 2010, Cameron announced that _Titanic_ would be converted and re-released in 3D to commemorate the centennial anniversary of the tragedy.[[97]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-97) On March 27, 2012, _[Titanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanic\\_(1997\\_film)#3D\\_conversion \"Titanic (1997 film)\")_ 3D premiered at London's [Royal Albert Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Albert_Hall \"Royal Albert Hall\").[[98]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-98) He also served as executive producer of _[Cirque du Soleil: Worlds Away](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cirque\\_du\\_Soleil:\\_Worlds\\_Away \"Cirque du Soleil: Worlds Away\")_ and _Deepsea Challenge 3D_ in 2012 and 2014, respectively.[[99]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-99)[[100]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-100)\n\n[![Image 10](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/77/James_Cameron_October_2012.jpg/250px-James_Cameron_October_2012.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:James_Cameron_October_2012.jpg)\n\nCameron in October 2012\n\nCameron starred in the 2017 documentary _Atlantis Rising,_ with collaborator [Simcha Jacobovici](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simcha_Jacobovici \"Simcha Jacobovici\"). The pair goes on an adventure to explore the existence of the city of [Atlantis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantis \"Atlantis\"). The programme aired on January 29 on National Geographic.[[101]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-101) Next, Cameron produced and appeared in a documentary about the [history of science fiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_science_fiction \"History of science fiction\"). _James Cameron's Story of Science Fiction_, the six-episodic series was broadcast on [AMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMC_(TV_channel) \"AMC (TV channel)\") in 2018.[[102]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-102) The series featured interviews with guests including [Ridley Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ridley_Scott \"Ridley Scott\"), [Steven Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Spielberg \"Steven Spielberg\"), [George Lucas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Lucas \"George Lucas\") and [Christopher Nolan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Nolan \"Christopher Nolan\").[[103]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-103) He stated: \"Without [Jules Verne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Verne \"Jules Verne\") and [H. G. Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H._G._Wells \"H. G. Wells\"), there wouldn't have been [Ray Bradbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Bradbury \"Ray Bradbury\") or [Robert A. Heinlein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_A._Heinlein \"Robert A. Heinlein\"), and without them, there wouldn't be [George] Lucas, [Steven] [Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Spielberg \"Steven Spielberg\"), Ridley Scott or me\".[[104]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-104)\n\n_[Alita: Battle Angel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alita:\\_Battle\\_Angel \"Alita: Battle Angel\")_ was finally released in 2019, after being in parallel development with _Avatar_. Written by Cameron and friend Jon Landau, the film was directed by [Robert Rodriguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Rodriguez \"Robert Rodriguez\") and produced by Cameron.[[105]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-105) The film is based on a 1990s Japanese [manga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manga \"Manga\") series _[Battle Angel Alita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle\\_Angel\\_Alita \"Battle Angel Alita\"),_ depicting a cyborg who cannot remember anything of her past life and tries to uncover the truth. Produced with similar techniques and technology as in _Avatar_, the film starred [Rosa Salazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosa_Salazar \"Rosa Salazar\"), [Christoph Waltz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christoph_Waltz \"Christoph Waltz\"), [Jennifer Connelly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Connelly \"Jennifer Connelly\"), [Mahershala Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahershala_Ali \"Mahershala Ali\"), [Ed Skrein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Skrein \"Ed Skrein\"), [Jackie Earle Haley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackie_Earle_Haley \"Jackie Earle Haley\") and [Keean Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keean_Johnson \"Keean Johnson\"). The film premiered on January 31, 2019, to generally positive reviews and $404 million (equivalent to $487,500,000 in 2024) at the worldwide box office.[[106]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-106) In her review, Monica Castillo of _[RogerEbert.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RogerEbert.com \"RogerEbert.com\")_ called it \"an awe-inspiring jump for [Rodriguez]\" and \"a visual bonanza\", despite the bulky script.[[107]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-107) Cameron then returned to the _Terminator_ franchise as producer and writer for [Tim Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Miller_(director) \"Tim Miller (director)\")'s _[Terminator: Dark Fate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminator:\\_Dark\\_Fate \"Terminator: Dark Fate\")_ (2019).[[108]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-108)\n\nIn August 2013, Cameron announced plans to direct three sequels to _Avatar_ simultaneously, for release in December 2016, 2017, and 2018.[[109]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-109) However, the release dates were adjusted due to Cameron's other priorities, with _Avatar_ _3_, _4_ and _5_ to be released, respectively, on December 20, 2024, December 18, 2026 and December 22, 2028.[[110]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-110)_[Deadline Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deadline\\_Hollywood \"Deadline Hollywood\")_ estimated that the budget for these would be over $1 billion.[[111]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-111)_Avatar 2_ (later given the subtitle _[The Way of Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar:\\_The\\_Way\\_of\\_Water \"Avatar: The Way of Water\")_) and _Avatar 3_ (later given the subtitle _[Fire and Ash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar:\\_Fire\\_and\\_Ash \"Avatar: Fire and Ash\")_) began [simultaneous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back-to-back_film_production \"Back-to-back film production\") production in [Manhattan Beach, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Beach,_California \"Manhattan Beach, California\") on August 15, 2017. Principal photography began in [New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand \"New Zealand\") on September 25, 2017.[[a]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-118) Parts of _Avatar 4_ were also filmed during this time.[[118]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-119) Cameron stated in a 2017 interview: \"Let's face it, if _Avatar 2_ and _3_ don't make enough money, there's not going to be a _4_ and _5_\".[[119]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-120)_Avatar: The Way of Water_ had its world premiere in London on December 6, 2022.[[120]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-121) It became the highest-grossing film released in 2022, and as of 2023 stood as the 3rd highest-grossing film of all time, behind only _Avatar_ and _[Avengers: Endgame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avengers:\\_Endgame \"Avengers: Endgame\")_, and just ahead of _Titanic_.[[121]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-122)[[122]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-123)\n\nLightstorm Entertainment bought the film rights to the [Taylor Stevens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Stevens \"Taylor Stevens\") novel _[The Informationist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Informationist \"The Informationist\")_, a thriller set in Africa with Cameron planning to direct.[[123]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-124) In 2010, he indicated he would adapt the [Charles R. Pellegrino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_R._Pellegrino \"Charles R. Pellegrino\") book _[The Last Train from Hiroshima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Last\\_Train\\_from\\_Hiroshima \"The Last Train from Hiroshima\")_, which is about the survivors of the [atomic bombings of Hiroshima and Nagasaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomic_bombings_of_Hiroshima_and_Nagasaki \"Atomic bombings of Hiroshima and Nagasaki\"). Cameron met with survivor [Tsutomu Yamaguchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsutomu_Yamaguchi \"Tsutomu Yamaguchi\") before his death in 2010.[[124]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-125) In 2024, _[Deadline Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deadline\\_Hollywood \"Deadline Hollywood\")_ confirmed that Cameron had purchased the rights of not only _The Last Train from Hiroshima_, but also of Pellegrino's forthcoming _Ghosts of Hiroshima_, to make an \"uncompromising theatrical epic motion picture\" titled _Last Train From Hiroshima_ about a Japanese man who survives Hiroshima's bombing at the height of [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II \"World War II\") only to then take a train to Nagasaki's bombing, which he will shoot as soon as the _Avatar_ sequels' production permits. Feeling that he and Pellegrino owe Yamaguchi for handing the baton of his personal story to them so they could pass his unique and harrowing experience to future generations, Cameron was assisted by the _Avatar_ sequels co-writer [Shane Salerno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shane_Salerno \"Shane Salerno\") and Pellegrino, who previously served as Cameron's science consultant on _Titanic_ and _Avatar_.[[125]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-CameronsNextNonAvatarFilm!-126)\n\nIn 2025, Cameron announced that Lightstorm Entertainment had acquired the rights to [Joe Abercrombie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Abercrombie \"Joe Abercrombie\")'s novel _The Devils_ and that he would begin working on a screenplay for a film based on the novel after completion of _Avatar: Fire and Ash_.[[126]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-127) In November 2025, Cameron directed _[Billie Eilish – Hit Me Hard and Soft: The Tour (Live in 3D)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billie\\_Eilish\\_%E2%80%93\\_Hit\\_Me\\_Hard\\_and\\_Soft:\\_The\\_Tour\\_(Live\\_in\\_3D) \"Billie Eilish – Hit Me Hard and Soft: The Tour (Live in 3D)\")_, a [concert film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concert_film \"Concert film\") documenting [Billie Eilish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billie_Eilish \"Billie Eilish\")'s _[Hit Me Hard and Soft: The Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hit\\_Me\\_Hard\\_and\\_Soft:\\_The\\_Tour \"Hit Me Hard and Soft: The Tour\")_.[[127]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-128)\n\n### Deep-sea and space exploration\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=7 \"Edit section: Deep-sea and space exploration\")]\n\nCameron has experience with [deep-sea exploration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep-sea_exploration \"Deep-sea exploration\"), in part because of his work on _The Abyss,_ _Titanic_, and _Avatar: The Way of Water_[[85]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-guardian2010-85) and his childhood fascination with shipwrecks. He has contributed to advancements in [underwater filming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underwater_videography \"Underwater videography\") and [remotely operated vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remotely_operated_underwater_vehicle \"Remotely operated underwater vehicle\"), and helped develop the 3D [Fusion Camera System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fusion_Camera_System \"Fusion Camera System\").[[128]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-129)[[129]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-130)[[130]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-131) In 2011, Cameron became a _[National Geographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National\\_Geographic \"National Geographic\")_ explorer-in-residence.[[131]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-132) In this role, on March 7, 2012, he dived five miles deep to the bottom of the [New Britain Trench](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon_Sea#Deepest_point \"Solomon Sea\") with the _[Deepsea Challenger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deepsea\\_Challenger \"Deepsea Challenger\")_.[[132]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-133) 19 days later, Cameron reached the [Challenger Deep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Challenger_Deep \"Challenger Deep\"), the deepest part of the [Mariana Trench](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariana_Trench \"Mariana Trench\").[[133]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-NGS-20120325-134)[[134]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-135)[[135]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-136) He spent more than three hours exploring the ocean floor, becoming the first to accomplish the trip alone.[[133]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-NGS-20120325-134)[[136]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-137) During his dive to the Challenger Deep, he discovered new species of [sea cucumber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_cucumber \"Sea cucumber\"), [squid worm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teuthidodrilus \"Teuthidodrilus\") and a giant single-celled [amoeba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amoeba \"Amoeba\").[[137]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-138) He was preceded by unmanned dives in [1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaik%C5%8D_ROV \"Kaikō ROV\") and [2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nereus_(underwater_vehicle) \"Nereus (underwater vehicle)\"), as well as by [Jacques Piccard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacques_Piccard \"Jacques Piccard\") and [Don Walsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Walsh \"Don Walsh\"), the first men to reach the bottom of the Mariana Trench aboard the [bathyscaphe _Trieste_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trieste_(bathyscaphe) \"Trieste (bathyscaphe)\") in 1960.[[138]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-139)\n\nIn the aftermath of the [_Titan_ submersible implosion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titan_submersible_implosion \"Titan submersible implosion\"), Cameron said he was \"struck by the similarity\" between the submersible's implosion and the events that resulted in the _Titanic_ disaster. He noted that both disasters seemed preventable, and were caused indirectly by someone deliberately ignoring safety warnings from others.[[139]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-140) Cameron criticized the company [OceanGate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OceanGate \"OceanGate\") and its late CEO [Stockton Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockton_Rush \"Stockton Rush\") for their choice of [carbon-fibre composite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon-fibre_composite \"Carbon-fibre composite\") construction of the pressure vessel, saying it has \"no strength in compression\" when subject to the immense pressures at depth.[[140]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Broad20232-141) Cameron said that pressure hulls should be made out of contiguous materials such as steel, titanium, ceramic, or acrylic, and that the wound carbon fibre of _Titan_'s hull had seemed like a bad idea to him from the beginning.[[141]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-slams2-142) He stated that it was long known that composite hulls were vulnerable to microscopic water ingress, delamination, and progressive failure over time.[[141]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-slams2-142) He also criticized Rush's real-time monitoring of the hull as an inadequate solution that would do little to prevent an implosion.[[140]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Broad20232-141) Cameron expressed regret for not being more outspoken about these concerns before the accident,[[141]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-slams2-142) and criticized what he termed \"false hopes\" being presented to the victims' families; he and his colleagues realized early on that for communication and tracking (the latter housed in a separate pressure vessel, with its own battery) to be lost simultaneously, the cause was almost certainly a catastrophic implosion.[[142]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-143)\n\nIn 2016, [Premier Exhibitions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_Exhibitions \"Premier Exhibitions\"), owner of many RMS _Titanic_ artifacts, filed for bankruptcy. Cameron supported the UK's [National Maritime Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Maritime_Museum \"National Maritime Museum\") and [National Museums Northern Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museums_Northern_Ireland \"National Museums Northern Ireland\") decision to bid for the artifacts, but they were acquired by an investment group before a formal bid took place.[[143]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-144)[[144]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-145)\n\nCameron also expressed a strong interest in visiting the space stations [Mir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mir \"Mir\") and [International Space Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Space_Station \"International Space Station\") (ISS).[[145]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-GQ1-146) He spent the summer of 2000 in Moscow getting ready for a potential trip to space, and was offered an opportunity to go by NASA.[[145]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-GQ1-146)[[146]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-147) However, the trip did not include a visit to the space station, so he declined the offer as it did not align with his terms. The shuttle flight he turned down was the tragic [Space Shuttle Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Shuttle_Columbia \"Space Shuttle Columbia\"). Cameron attended the memorial service for the victims of the [disaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Shuttle_Columbia_disaster \"Space Shuttle Columbia disaster\").[[145]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-GQ1-146)\n\n## Legal issues\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=8 \"Edit section: Legal issues\")]\n\nIn June 2013, British artist [Roger Dean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Dean_(artist) \"Roger Dean (artist)\") filed a copyright complaint against Cameron, seeking damages of $50 million (equivalent to $66,200,000 in 2024). The lawsuit stated that Cameron used and referenced Dean's designs in _AVATAR_, accusing the filmmaker of \"wilful and deliberate copying, dissemination and exploitation\" of Dean's original images. The case was dismissed by US district judge [Jesse Furman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesse_M._Furman \"Jesse M. Furman\") in 2014.[[147]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-148)[[148]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-149)\n\n## Personal life\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=9 \"Edit section: Personal life\")]\n\nCameron has been married five times.[[149]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-150) He was married to Sharon Williams from 1978 to 1984. A year after he and Williams divorced, Cameron married film producer [Gale Anne Hurd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gale_Anne_Hurd \"Gale Anne Hurd\"), a close collaborator for his 1980s films. They divorced in 1989. Soon after separating from Hurd, Cameron met the director [Kathryn Bigelow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathryn_Bigelow \"Kathryn Bigelow\"), whom he wed in 1989; they divorced in 1991. Cameron then began a relationship with [Linda Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linda_Hamilton \"Linda Hamilton\"), the lead actress in _The Terminator_ series. Their daughter was born in 1993. Cameron married Hamilton in 1997. Amid speculation of an affair between Cameron and actress [Suzy Amis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzy_Amis_Cameron \"Suzy Amis Cameron\"), Cameron and Hamilton separated after two years of marriage, with Hamilton receiving a settlement of $50 million.[[150]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-151)[[151]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-152)[[152]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-153) He married Amis, his fifth wife, in 2000. They have one son and two daughters together.[[153]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-154)\n\nCameron applied for American citizenship in 2004, but withdrew his application after [George W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_W._Bush \"George W. Bush\") won the presidential election.[[154]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-155) Cameron resided in the United States, but after filming _Avatar_ in New Zealand, Cameron bought a home and a farm there in 2012.[[155]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-156)[[156]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-157)[[157]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-158) He divided his time between [Malibu, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malibu,_California \"Malibu, California\") and New Zealand until 2020,[[158]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-159) after which he sold his Malibu home and decided to live in New Zealand permanently.[[159]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-160) He said in August 2020: \"I plan to make all my future films in New Zealand, and I see the country having an opportunity to demonstrate to the international film industry how to safely return to work. Doing so with _Avatar_ [sequels] will be a beacon that, when this is over [[COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic \"COVID-19 pandemic\")], will attract more production to New Zealand and continue to stimulate the screen industry and the economy for years.\"[[160]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-161)[[161]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-162) In February 2025, Cameron was planning to formally become a New Zealand citizen.[[162]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-163)[[163]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-164) He was formally granted New Zealand citizenship at a ceremony on August 13, 2025.[[164]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-165)\n\nCameron is an [atheist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism \"Atheism\"); he formerly associated himself with [agnosticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnosticism \"Agnosticism\"), a stance he said he had come to see as \"cowardly atheism.\"[[63]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Seering-63) Since 2011, he is [vegan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veganism \"Veganism\").[[165]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-166) Cameron met close friend [Guillermo del Toro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillermo_del_Toro \"Guillermo del Toro\") on the production of his 1993 film, _[Cronos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cronos\\_(film) \"Cronos (film)\")_.[[166]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-DelToroRansom-167) In 1997, del Toro's father Federico was kidnapped in [Guadalajara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guadalajara \"Guadalajara\") and Cameron gave del Toro more than $1 million (equivalent to $1,790,000 in 2024) in cash to pay a ransom and have his father released.[[166]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-DelToroRansom-167)[[167]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-168)[[168]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-169) Cameron had been friends with _Titanic_ expert [Paul-Henri Nargeolet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul-Henri_Nargeolet \"Paul-Henri Nargeolet\") for over 25 years before the latter's death.[[169]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-170)\n\nCameron joined the board of directors of AI company [Stability AI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stability_AI \"Stability AI\") in September 2024.[[170]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-171)\n\n## Filmmaking style\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=10 \"Edit section: Filmmaking style\")]\n\n### Themes\n\n[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=James_Cameron&action=edit&section=11 \"Edit section: Themes\")]\n\n[![Image 11](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Avatar_The_Way_of_Water_Tokyo_Press_Conference_James_Cameron_%2852563430565%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Avatar_The_Way_of_Water_Tokyo_Press_Conference_James_Cameron_%2852563430565%29_%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Avatar_The_Way_of_Water_Tokyo_Press_Conference_James_Cameron_(52563430565)_(cropped).jpg)\n\nCameron in 2022\n\nCameron's films are often based on themes which explore the conflicts between [intelligent machines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_intelligence \"Artificial intelligence\") and humanity or [nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature \"Nature\"),[[171]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-172)[[172]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-173) dangers of corporate greed,[[173]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-174) strong female characters, and a romance subplot.[[174]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-175) Cameron has further stated in an interview with The Talks: \"All my movies are love stories\".[[175]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-176) Both _[Titanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanic\\_(1997\\_film) \"Titanic (1997 film)\")_ and _[Avatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar\\_(2009\\_film) \"Avatar (2009 film)\")_ are noted for featuring [star-crossed lovers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star-crossed_lovers \"Star-crossed lovers\").[[176]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-177) The _Avatar_ franchise also explores themes like family dynamics and grief.[[177]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron#cite_note-Collider12.5.25-178) Characters suffering from emotionally intense and dramatic environments in the sea wilderness are explored in _[The Abyss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The\\_Abyss \"The Abyss\")_ and _Titanic_. The _[Terminator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminator\\_(franchise) \"Terminator (franchise)\")_ series amplifies technology as an enemy which could lead to devastation of mankind.
